      "- claude: Catch-all for any task that doesn't fit a more specific agent. FleetView's default when no agent name is typed. (Tools: *)",
      "- Explore: Fast read-only search agent for locating code. Use it to find files by pattern (eg. \"src/components/**/*.tsx\"), grep for symbols or keywords (eg. \"API endpoints\"), or answer \"where is X defined / which files reference Y.\" Do NOT use it for code review, design-doc auditing, cross-file consistency checks, or open-ended analysis — it reads excerpts rather than whole files and will miss content past its read window. When calling, specify search breadth: \"quick\" for a single targeted lookup, \"medium\" for moderate exploration, or \"very thorough\" to search across multiple locations and naming conventions. (Tools: All tools except Agent, ExitPlanMode, Edit, Write, NotebookEdit)",
      "- general-purpose: General-purpose agent for researching complex questions, searching for code, and executing multi-step tasks.       "- Plan: Software architect agent for designing implementation plans. Use this when you need to plan the implementation strategy for a task.     "type": "skill_listing",
    "content": "- update-config: Use this skill to configure the Claude Code harness via settings.json. Automated behaviors (\"from now on when X\", \"each time X\", \"whenever X\", \"before/after X\") require hooks configured in settings.json - the harness executes these, not Claude, so memory/preferences cannot fulfill them. Also use for: permissions (\"allow X\", \"add permission\", \"move permission to\"), env vars (\"set X=Y\"), hook troubleshooting, or any changes to settings.json/settings.local.json files. Examples: \"allow npm commands\", \"add bq permission to global settings\", \"move permission to user settings\", \"set DEBUG=true\", \"when claude stops show X\". For simple settings like theme/model, suggest the /config command.\n- keybindings-help: Use when the user wants to customize keyboard shortcuts, rebind keys, add chord bindings, or modify ~/.claude/keybindings.json. Examples: \"rebind ctrl+s\", \"add a chord shortcut\", \"change the submit key\", \"customize keybindings\".\n- simplify: Review changed code for reuse, quality, and efficiency, then fix any issues found.\n- fewer-permission-prompts: Scan your transcripts for common read-only Bash and MCP tool calls, then add a prioritized allowlist to project .claude/settings.json to reduce permission prompts.\n- loop: Run a prompt or slash command on a recurring interval (e.g. /loop 5m /foo). Omit the interval to let the model self-pace. - When the user wants to set up a recurring task, poll for status, or run something repeatedly on an interval (e.g. \"check the deploy every 5 minutes\", \"keep running /babysit-prs\"). Do NOT invoke for one-off tasks.\n- schedule: Create, update, list, or run scheduled remote agents (routines) that execute on a cron schedule. - When the user wants to schedule a recurring remote agent, set up automated tasks, create a cron job for Claude Code, or manage their scheduled agents/routines. Also use when the user wants a one-time scheduled run (\"run this once at 3pm\", \"remind me to check X tomorrow\").\n- claude-api: Build, debug, and optimize Claude API / Anthropic SDK apps. Apps built with this skill should include prompt caching. Also handles migrating existing Claude API code between Claude model versions (4.5 → 4.6, 4.6 → 4.7, retired-model replacements).\nTRIGGER when: code imports `anthropic`/`@anthropic-ai/sdk`; user asks for the Claude API, Anthropic SDK, or Managed Agents; user adds/modifies/tunes a Claude feature (caching, thinking, compaction, tool use, batch, files, citations, memory) or model (Opus/Sonnet/Haiku) in a file; questions about prompt caching / cache hit rate in an Anthropic SDK project.\nSKIP: file imports `openai`/other-provider SDK, filename like `*-openai.py`/`*-generic.py`, provider-neutral code, general programming/ML.\n- init: Initialize a new CLAUDE.md file with codebase documentation\n- review: Review a pull request\n- security-review: Complete a security review of the pending changes on the current branch",

{"result":"{\"success\": true, \"data\": {\"path\": \"/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0509_6/projects/trace-verdict-study/ROADMAP.md\", \"content\": \"# trace-verdict-study — Research Dashboard\\n\\n> 最后更新: 2026-05-12 11:26 UTC | 更新原因: D004 阻塞解除，MVP 进入执行阶段\\n\\n## 核心方向\\nProvUIE: 将 evidence-span co-prediction 作为 GRPO 可验证奖励，训练 LLM 在做 UIE 时同步输出证据跨度。\\n核心差异化：ProUIE SFR = 结构抽取正确性，ProvUIE 新增 evidence provenance 维度（NLI entailment + IoU）。\\n术语：统一使用 \\\"evidence provenance reward\\\"，不用 \\\"faithfulness reward\\\"（避免与 MR-UIE 混淆）。\\n\\n## 当前进展\\n- 阶段: MVP 验证 → 执行中\\n- 文献验证: ✅ 全部完成（ProUIE/R2GRPO/MR-UIE 差异化均成立）\\n- D003 阻塞: ✅ 已解除（MR-UIE faithfulness ≠ evidence provenance，D004）\\n- GPU: ✅ 2×RTX PRO 6000 (96GB) @ autodl-rtxpro6000\\n- Agent: 正在消化 worker 队列，综合指令已排队\\n\\n## MVP 执行进度\\n- [ ] Step 0: 环境搭建（autodl-rtxpro6000）\\n- [ ] Step 1: DocRED 数据准备\\n- [ ] Step 2: SFT baseline → 确定 Evi F1 锚点\\n- [ ] Step 3: Post-hoc NLI reranking\\n- [ ] Step 4: GRPO with evidence provenance reward\\n- [ ] Step 5: 三组对比 + degeneration sanity checks\\n\\n## Kill Criteria（三档）\\n- Kill: Evi F1(GRPO) - Evi F1(post-hoc) < 2pt，或 RE F1 回归 > 3pt\\n- Inconclusive: 差值 2-4pt → 换 seed 重跑\\n- Pass: 差值 ≥ 5pt，且 RE F1 回归 ≤ 2pt\\n\\n## GPU 预算\\n- 已分配: 2×RTX PRO 6000 (96GB) @ autodl-rtxpro6000\\n- MVP 估计: ~20-30 GPU-hours（48GB→96GB 后 group_size 可放大，但 RTX PRO vs H100 算力差异，略上调估算）\\n- 全项目: ≤200 GPU-hours\\n\\n## 关键决策日志\\n\\n| ID | 日期 | 摘要 | 关联实验 |\\n|----|------|------|----------|\\n| D004 | 2026-05-12 | D003 阻塞解除：MR-UIE faithfulness ≠ ProvUIE evidence p | — |\\n| D003 | 2026-05-12 | MR-UIE faithfulness reward 精读升级为阻塞性前置，与 ProUIE 同级 | — |\\n| D002 | 2026-05-12 | 接受 Reviewer critical：MVP 加入 post-hoc NLI reranking | — |\\n| D001 | 2026-05-12 | 立项启动 MVP，选 DocRED + Qwen3-8B 验证 provenance fidelit | — |\\n\\n\\n\", \"lines\": 45}, \"error\": null, \"metadata\": {}}"}

2. **RE-F1 is quite low (~0.28)**: This is concerning. The running F1 has been declining from 0.32 at batch 56 to 0.28 at batch 504. 

3. **GPU 1 has an unregistered process (PID 58729)**: This is a generation config test script comparing TRL defaults vs model defaults. It uses `CUDA_VISIBLE_DEVICES=1`. It's NOT registered in the registry. This was started at 06:52.

4. **GPU allocation check**: The registry shows sft_baseline_v3 used `cuda:0` and `cuda:1`, and the ROADMAP says "2×RTX PRO 6000 (96GB) @ autodl-rtxpro6000". The F1 score might recover as training continues, but it's worth monitoring.

5. **Tokenizer and generation config issues**: There's a Mistral tokenizer regex warning and a `torch_dtype` deprecation, but more critically, the generation flags for temperature, top_p, and top_k are being ignored during evaluation. This means the model's generating with default settings instead of the intended configuration, which could skew the results.

Let me verify the gpu_ids configuration in the project registry to confirm GPU boundary compliance.

Looking at sft_eval_v4's spec, it's set to `cuda:0` with `CUDA_VISIBLE_DEVICES=0`, which is correct. The unregistered process on GPU 1 (PID 58729) appears to be from a status check worker that just completed, not the main generation test. The eval itself is progressing fine at 504/985 (~51%), the RE-F1 metric of ~0.28 is lower than expected but still a live metric, and that unregistered process is likely a debugging script that wasn't properly registered in the system.

The real concern is the generation flags warning — temperature, top_p, and top_k are being marked as invalid and potentially ignored by the evaluate.py script, which could seriously compromise the eval results if the model.generate() call isn't actually using these sampling parameters as intended.

Actually, for evaluation purposes this might be acceptable since F1 scoring typically requires deterministic greedy generation anyway, so ignoring those sampling flags wouldn't be problematic. But it's worth noting.

The worker that completed (worker_ssh_urgent-status_20260512T225001) appears to have been an emergency intervention — the Agent likely detected the hung v3 and posthoc_nli_v1 jobs, terminated them, then launched v4 with the batch and max_prompt_length fixes, plus a debug script on GPU 1 to investigate the generation config issue. That's a reasonable response to the situation. Now I need to verify the GPU allocation boundaries are being respected correctly.

Looking at the roadmap, both RTX PRO 6000 GPUs (96GB each) are allocated, so I should check the project config to see which specific gpu_ids are assigned.

{"result":"{\"success\": true, \"data\": {\"stdout\": \"\\\"\\\"\\\"GRPO training for ProvUIE.\\n\\nContinues from a merged SFT checkpoint with Group Relative Policy Optimization.\\nReward = weighted(provenance_fidelity, structural_f1, format_compliance).\\n\\nInput:  Merged SFT model + JSONL with prompt + gold metadata.\\nOutput: GRPO-tuned model checkpoint.\\n\\nUsage:\\n    python train_grpo.py --help\\n    python train_grpo.py --sft_checkpoint ./outputs/sft/merged\\n\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n\\nimport torch\\nfrom transformers import AutoTokenizer, HfArgumentParser\\nfrom trl import GRPOConfig, GRPOTrainer\\nfrom peft import LoraConfig, TaskType\\n\\nfrom config import ModelArgs, GRPOArgs, DataArgs, RewardArgs\\nfrom data_utils import load_grpo_dataset\\nfrom rewards import composite_reward\\n\\n\\ndef main():\\n    parser = HfArgumentParser((ModelArgs, GRPOArgs, DataArgs, RewardArgs))\\n    model_args, grpo_args, data_args, reward_args = parser.parse_args_into_dataclasses()\\n\\n    # ── Tokenizer ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────\\n    # Left-padding for generation in GRPO\\n    tokenizer = AutoTokenizer.from_pretrained(\\n        grpo_args.sft_checkpoint,\\n        trust_remote_code=True,\\n        padding_side=\\\"left\\\",\\n    )\\n    if tokenizer.pad_token is None:\\n        tokenizer.pad_token = tokenizer.eos_token\\n\\n    # ── Model kwargs (passed to HF model via GRPOConfig.model_init_kwargs) ──\\n    # In colocate mode, GRPOTrainer loads the HF model internally — we pass\\n    # the checkpoint path as a string, not a pre-loaded model object.\\n    model_kwargs: dict = {\\\"trust_remote_code\\\": True, \\\"torch_dtype\\\": torch.bfloat16}\\n    if model_args.use_flash_attention:\\n        model_kwargs[\\\"attn_implementation\\\"] = \\\"flash_attention_2\\\"\\n\\n    # ── Fresh LoRA adapter for GRPO ────────────────────────────────────\\n    peft_config = LoraConfig(\\n        task_type=TaskType.CAUSAL_LM,\\n        r=model_args.lora_rank,\\n        lora_alpha=model_args.lora_alpha,\\n        lora_dropout=model_args.lora_dropout,\\n        target_modules=model_args.get_target_modules(),\\n    )\\n\\n    # ── TRL GRPO config ────────────────────────────────────────────────\\n    training_config = GRPOConfig(\\n        output_dir=grpo_args.output_dir,\\n        num_generations=grpo_args.num_generations,\\n        temperature=grpo_args.temperature,\\n        top_k=grpo_args.top_k,\\n        top_p=grpo_args.top_p,\\n        beta=grpo_args.beta,\\n        learning_rate=grpo_args.learning_rate,\\n        num_train_epochs=grpo_args.num_train_epochs,\\n        max_steps=grpo_args.max_steps,\\n        per_device_train_batch_size=grpo_args.per_device_train_batch_size,\\n        gradient_accumulation_steps=grpo_args.gradient_accumulation_steps,\\n        max_completion_length=grpo_args.max_completion_length,\\n        logging_steps=grpo_args.logging_steps,\\n        save_steps=grpo_args.save_steps,\\n        save_total_limit=grpo_args.save_total_limit,\\n        bf16=grpo_args.bf16,\\n        report_to=grpo_args.report_to,\\n        run_name=grpo_args.run_name,\\n        gradient_checkpointing=grpo_args.gradient_checkpointing,\\n        gradient_checkpointing_kwargs={\\\"use_reentrant\\\": False},\\n        model_init_kwargs=model_kwargs,\\n        chat_template_kwargs={\\\"enable_thinking\\\": False},\\n    )\\n\\n    # ── Dataset ────────────────────────────────────────────────────────\\n    train_ds = load_grpo_dataset(data_args.train_file, max_samples=data_args.max_train_samples)\\n    print(f\\\"GRPO train samples: {len(train_ds)}\\\")\\n\\n    # ── Reward function (capture configured weights) ───────────────────\\n    def reward_fn(prompts, completions, **kwargs):\\n        kwargs.update({\\n            \\\"w_provenance\\\": reward_args.w_provenance,\\n            \\\"w_structural\\\": reward_args.w_structural,\\n            \\\"w_format\\\": reward_args.w_format,\\n            \\\"nli_batch_size\\\": reward_args.nli_batch_size,\\n            \\\"nli_model_name\\\": reward_args.nli_model_name,\\n            \\\"nli_device\\\": reward_args.nli_device,\\n            \\\"length_penalty_factor\\\": reward_args.length_penalty_factor,\\n            \\\"iou_weight\\\": reward_args.iou_weight,\\n            \\\"nli_weight\\\": reward_args.nli_weight,\\n            \\\"length_weight\\\": reward_args.length_weight,\\n        })\\n        return composite_reward(prompts, completions, **kwargs)\\n\\n    # ── Trainer ────────────────────────────────────────────────────────\\n    trainer = GRPOTrainer(\\n        model=grpo_args.sft_checkpoint,\\n        reward_funcs=[reward_fn],\\n        args=training_config,\\n        train_dataset=train_ds,\\n        processing_class=tokenizer,\\n        peft_config=peft_config,\\n    )\\n\\n    trainer.train()\\n\\n    # ── Save ───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────\\n    final_dir = f\\\"{grpo_args.output_dir}/final\\\"\\n    trainer.save_model(final_dir)\\n    tokenizer.save_pretrained(final_dir)\\n    print(f\\\"GRPO model saved to {final_dir}\\\")\\n\\n\\nif __name__ == \\\"__main__\\\":\\n    main()\\n\", \"stderr\": \"\", \"return_code\": 0}, \"error\": null, \"metadata\": {\"elapsed_seconds\": 0.53}}"}
```

      "result": "{\"success\": true, \"data\": {\"stdout\": \"\\\"\\\"\\\"GRPO training for ProvUIE.\\n\\nContinues from a merged SFT checkpoint with Group Relative Policy Optimization.\\nReward = weighted(provenance_fidelity, structural_f1, format_compliance).\\n\\nInput:  Merged SFT model + JSONL with prompt + gold metadata.\\nOutput: GRPO-tuned model checkpoint.\\n\\nUsage:\\n    python train_grpo.py --help\\n    python train_grpo.py --sft_checkpoint ./outputs/sft/merged\\n\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n\\nimport torch\\nfrom transformers import AutoTokenizer, HfArgumentParser\\nfrom trl import GRPOConfig, GRPOTrainer\\nfrom peft import LoraConfig, TaskType\\n\\nfrom config import ModelArgs, GRPOArgs, DataArgs, RewardArgs\\nfrom data_utils import load_grpo_dataset\\nfrom rewards import composite_reward\\n\\n\\ndef main():\\n    parser = HfArgumentParser((ModelArgs, GRPOArgs, DataArgs, RewardArgs))\\n    model_args, grpo_args, data_args, reward_args = parser.parse_args_into_dataclasses()\\n\\n    # ── Tokenizer ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────\\n    # Left-padding for generation in GRPO\\n    tokenizer = AutoTokenizer.from_pretrained(\\n        grpo_args.sft_checkpoint,\\n        trust_remote_code=True,\\n        padding_side=\\\"left\\\",\\n    )\\n    if tokenizer.pad_token is None:\\n        tokenizer.pad_token = tokenizer.eos_token\\n\\n    # ── Model kwargs (passed to HF model via GRPOConfig.model_init_kwargs) ──\\n    # In colocate mode, GRPOTrainer loads the HF model internally — we pass\\n    # the checkpoint path as a string, not a pre-loaded model object.\\n    model_kwargs: dict = {\\\"trust_remote_code\\\": True, \\\"torch_dtype\\\": torch.bfloat16}\\n    if model_args.use_flash_attention:\\n        model_kwargs[\\\"attn_implementation\\\"] = \\\"flash_attention_2\\\"\\n\\n    # ── Fresh LoRA adapter for GRPO ────────────────────────────────────\\n    peft_config = LoraConfig(\\n        task_type=TaskType.CAUSAL_LM,\\n        r=model_args.lora_rank,\\n        lora_alpha=model_args.lora_alpha,\\n        lora_dropout=model_args.lora_dropout,\\n        target_modules=model_args.get_target_modules(),\\n    )\\n\\n    # ── TRL GRPO config ────────────────────────────────────────────────\\n    training_config = GRPOConfig(\\n        output_dir=grpo_args.output_dir,\\n        num_generations=grpo_args.num_generations,\\n        temperature=grpo_args.temperature,\\n        top_k=grpo_args.top_k,\\n        top_p=grpo_args.top_p,\\n        beta=grpo_args.beta,\\n        learning_rate=grpo_args.learning_rate,\\n        num_train_epochs=grpo_args.num_train_epochs,\\n        max_steps=grpo_args.max_steps,\\n        per_device_train_batch_size=grpo_args.per_device_train_batch_size,\\n        gradient_accumulation_steps=grpo_args.gradient_accumulation_steps,\\n        max_completion_length=grpo_args.max_completion_length,\\n        logging_steps=grpo_args.logging_steps,\\n        save_steps=grpo_args.save_steps,\\n        save_total_limit=grpo_args.save_total_limit,\\n        bf16=grpo_args.bf16,\\n        report_to=grpo_args.report_to,\\n        run_name=grpo_args.run_name,\\n        gradient_checkpointing=grpo_args.gradient_checkpointing,\\n        gradient_checkpointing_kwargs={\\\"use_reentrant\\\": False},\\n        model_init_kwargs=model_kwargs,\\n        chat_template_kwargs={\\\"enable_thinking\\\": False},\\n    )\\n\\n    # ── Dataset ────────────────────────────────────────────────────────\\n    train_ds = load_grpo_dataset(data_args.train_file, max_samples=data_args.max_train_samples)\\n    print(f\\\"GRPO train samples: {len(train_ds)}\\\")\\n\\n    # ── Reward function (capture configured weights) ───────────────────\\n    def reward_fn(prompts, completions, **kwargs):\\n        kwargs.update({\\n            \\\"w_provenance\\\": reward_args.w_provenance,\\n            \\\"w_structural\\\": reward_args.w_structural,\\n            \\\"w_format\\\": reward_args.w_format,\\n            \\\"nli_batch_size\\\": reward_args.nli_batch_size,\\n            \\\"nli_model_name\\\": reward_args.nli_model_name,\\n            \\\"nli_device\\\": reward_args.nli_device,\\n            \\\"length_penalty_factor\\\": reward_args.length_penalty_factor,\\n            \\\"iou_weight\\\": reward_args.iou_weight,\\n            \\\"nli_weight\\\": reward_args.nli_weight,\\n            \\\"length_weight\\\": reward_args.length_weight,\\n        })\\n        return composite_reward(prompts, completions, **kwargs)\\n\\n    # ── Trainer ────────────────────────────────────────────────────────\\n    trainer = GRPOTrainer(\\n        model=grpo_args.sft_checkpoint,\\n        reward_funcs=[reward_fn],\\n        args=training_config,\\n        train_dataset=train_ds,\\n        processing_class=tokenizer,\\n        peft_config=peft_config,\\n    )\\n\\n    trainer.train()\\n\\n    # ── Save ───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────\\n    final_dir = f\\\"{grpo_args.output_dir}/final\\\"\\n    trainer.save_model(final_dir)\\n    tokenizer.save_pretrained(final_dir)\\n    print(f\\\"GRPO model saved to {final_dir}\\\")\\n\\n\\nif __name__ == \\\"__main__\\\":\\n    main()\\n\", \"stderr\": \"\", \"return_code\": 0}, \"error\": null, \"metadata\": {\"elapsed_seconds\": 0.53}}"

_source line 90 · 2026-05-12T22:59:35.344Z_

<details><summary>Logged reasoning block</summary>

```text
Now I can see the key changes:

1. **SYSTEM_PROMPT with `/no_think`**: In `data_utils.py`, the system prompt ends with `\n/no_think`. This is Qwen3's way to disable thinking mode in generation. This is the "fix-thinking" worker's work.

2. **`train_grpo.py` with `chat_template_kwargs={"enable_thinking": False}`**: The GRPOConfig passes `enable_thinking: False` to the chat template. This is also the thinking mode fix.

3. **`config.py` GRPOArgs has `top_k: int = 20` and `top_p: float = 0.95`**: These are Qwen3's recommended defaults, different from TRL defaults (top_k=0, top_p=1.0). 

The core issue is that Qwen3-8B's thinking mode generates `&lt;think>...&lt;/think>` tokens before the actual response, which breaks the training pipeline. The fixes disable this across two places: the system prompt explicitly tells the model not to think, and the chat template configuration also turns off thinking mode to ensure clean output during GRPO training. I'm looking at the rewards.py file to understand how the reward computation works and ensure it aligns with the other configuration changes we've made.
```

2. **fix-thinking worker changes**:
   - `data_utils.py` SYSTEM_PROMPT: ends with `\n/no_think` — disables Qwen3 thinking mode via prompt-level control
   - `train_grpo.py`: `chat_template_kwargs={"enable_thinking": False}` — disables thinking at template level for GRPO training
   - `rewards.py`: `re.sub(r'&lt;think>.*?&lt;/think>', '', text, flags=re.DOTALL)` — strips thinking tags as fallback safety in reward parsing
   
3. The fix-jinja-template worker addressed the Jinja2 template rendering issue with Qwen3's tokenizer, ensuring the `enable_thinking` parameter integrates properly with TRL's chat template application.

4. The GRPOArgs configuration now uses Qwen3's recommended sampling parameters (top_k=20, top_p=0.95) instead of TRL's defaults, and these changes are all setting up the foundation for GRPO training — the thinking mode handling is especially critical since without it, GRPO completions would include unwanted thinking tags.

The three-layer defense prevents thinking content from contaminating the output: the system prompt blocks it at the prompt level, the chat template disables it during rendering, and the rewards parser strips any remaining thinking tokens. Now I'm checking on the eval progress — the log showed [504/985] earlier, so I need to see if it's moved forward since then. The debug process on GPU 1 has finished, and there are no registry violations or GPU boundary issues to worry about. I'll put together a brief status report.

Now I see what happened:

1. **chat_template.jinja was modified at 06:58** — the worker modified the chat template and kept a backup (`chat_template.jinja.bak` at 06:57, `tokenizer_config.json.bak` at 06:57). This is the "fix-thinking" part — they were fixing the Qwen3 thinking mode in the chat template.

2. **generation_config.json is unchanged** (modified 22:54 on May 12) — but it shows `do_sample: true, temperature: 0.6, top_k: 20, top_p: 0.95`. 

The sampling config in generation_config.json explains why the eval warning about temperature and top_p flags being ignored — evaluate.py is setting these parameters but they're being overridden by what's already in the model's config. Since the chat template was modified while sft_eval_v4 is still running on GPU 0, the currently executing eval won't be affected because the tokenizer was already loaded at startup, but any future evaluations will pick up the new template.

Now I'm checking what actually changed in the chat template — the "thinking" fix is probably disabling Qwen3's thinking mode that wraps outputs in special tags and interferes with structured output parsing. I should also look at what the modified Python files contain to understand the full scope of changes.

### Assistant

_source line 149 · 2026-05-12T23:02:04.679Z_

<details><summary>Logged reasoning block</summary>

````text
Now I can see the key difference:

**Original (bak):**
```jinja
{%- if add_generation_prompt %}
    {{- '<|im_start|>assistant\n' }}
    {%- if enable_thinking is defined and enable_thinking is false %}
        {{- '&lt;think>\n\n&lt;/think>\n\n' }}
    {%- endif %}
{%- endif %}
```

**Modified (new):**


Inserting the empty think block unconditionally...

The original only adds the empty think block when `enable_thinking` is explicitly set to false, but the new version always injects it whenever `add_generation_prompt` is true. This fundamentally changes the behavior — instead of optionally disabling thinking mode, it now forces an empty thinking block into every response.

This is actually the right fix for the hanging issue where the model gets stuck in extended reasoning chains. But there's a timing problem: sft_eval_v4 started before this template change was made, so it's still running with the old conditional logic. The evaluate.py script loads the tokenizer's chat template from disk at runtime, so I need to check whether it's picking up the updated template or if it cached the old one before the change.

The fact that sft_eval_v4 is still producing output (504/985 with RE-F1=0.28) suggests it's not completely hanging, but the low F1 score makes me wonder if thinking mode is still interfering—maybe the model sometimes enters thinking mode and sometimes doesn't, causing garbage in the output that breaks the RE parsing. I should look at the evaluate.py to see how it's handling generation and whether it's explicitly disabling thinking mode or stripping think blocks from the output.
````

./outputs/grpo/final --eval_file ./data/docred_dev.jsonl\\n\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n\\nimport os\\nos.environ.setdefault(\\\"PYTHONUNBUFFERED\\\", \\\"1\\\")\\n\\nimport json\\nfrom pathlib import But `enable_thinking` is NOT passed in the evaluate.py code, so the condition `enable_thinking is defined and enable_thinking is false` would be False (because `enable_thinking is not defined`). 

So with the old template, since `enable_thinking` isn't defined, the empty think block wouldn't be injected—the model would enter thinking mode and waste tokens generating reasoning before the answer, potentially hitting max_length before producing output. The new template fixes this by unconditionally injecting the empty think block to disable thinking, letting the model go straight to answering.

Now I need to check if sft_eval_v4 is actually using the old template. It started at 05:38 UTC+8, but the template fix was applied at 06:57-06:58 UTC+8, so yes, it loaded before the fix. {"result":"{\"success\": true, \"data\": {\"stdout\": \"\\\"\\\"\\\"Evaluation script for ProvUIE.\\n\\nGenerates predictions from a trained model and computes:\\n  - RE F1:  relation extraction F1 (exact triple match)\\n  - Evi F1: evidence sentence F1 (DocRED standard)\\n  - Attribution Precision: fraction of extractions backed by evidence\\n\\nInput:  Model checkpoint + evaluation JSONL.\\nOutput: Formatted report + JSON results file.\\n\\nChanges:\\n  - Batch generation via left-padded tokenization (--batch_size, default 8)\\n\\nUsage:\\n    python evaluate.py --model_path ./outputs/grpo/final --eval_file ./data/docred_dev.jsonl\\n\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n\\nimport os\\nos.environ.setdefault(\\\"PYTHONUNBUFFERED\\\", \\\"1\\\")\\n\\nimport json\\nfrom pathlib import Path\\n\\nimport torch\\nfrom transformers import AutoModelForCausalLM, AutoTokenizer, HfArgumentParser\\n\\nfrom config import ModelArgs, EvalArgs\\nfrom data_utils import load_grpo_dataset\\nfrom rewards import parse_json_output\\n\\n\\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\\n# Metrics\\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\\n\\ndef compute_re_f1(pred_rels: list[dict], gold_rels: list[dict]) -> tuple[float, float, float]:\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"Relation extraction F1 (exact triple match on head+tail+relation).\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n    pred_set = {\\n        (r.get(\\\"head\\\", \\\"\\\").lower(), r.get(\\\"tail\\\", \\\"\\\").lower(), r.get(\\\"relation\\\", \\\"\\\").lower())\\n        for r in pred_rels\\n    }\\n    gold_set = {\\n        (r[\\\"head\\\"].lower(), r[\\\"tail\\\"].lower(), r[\\\"relation\\\"].lower())\\n        for r in gold_rels\\n    }\\n    if not pred_set and not gold_set:\\n        return 1.0, 1.0, 1.0\\n\\n    tp = len(pred_set & gold_set)\\n    prec = tp / len(pred_set) if pred_set else 0.0\\n    rec = tp / len(gold_set) if gold_set else 0.0\\n    f1 = 2 * prec * rec / (prec + rec) if (prec + rec) > 0 else 0.0\\n    return prec, rec, f1\\n\\n\\ndef compute_evi_f1(\\n    pred_rels: list[dict], gold_rels: list[dict], sentences: list[str]\\n) -> tuple[float, float, float]:\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"Evidence sentence F1 (per-relation, sentence-level).\\n\\n    Matches predicted evidence text back to sentence indices for comparison\\n    with gold evidence_sids.\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n    sent_to_idx: dict[str, int] = {}\\n    for i, s in enumerate(sentences):\\n        sent_to_idx[s.strip().lower()] = i\\n\\n    all_pred: set[tuple] = set()\\n    all_gold: set[tuple] = set()\\n\\n    gold_map: dict[tuple, set[int]] = {}\\n    for r in gold_rels:\\n        key = (r[\\\"head\\\"].lower(), r[\\\"tail\\\"].lower(), r[\\\"relation\\\"].lower())\\n        sids = set(r.get(\\\"evidence_sids\\\", []))\\n        if not sids:\\n            for ev in r.get(\\\"evidence\\\", []):\\n                idx = sent_to_idx.get(ev.strip().lower())\\n                if idx is not None:\\n                    sids.add(idx)\\n        gold_map[key] = sids\\n        for sid in sids:\\n            all_gold.add((key, sid))\\n\\n    for r in pred_rels:\\n        key = (\\n            r.get(\\\"head\\\", \\\"\\\").lower(),\\n            r.get(\\\"tail\\\", \\\"\\\").lower(),\\n            r.get(\\\"relation\\\", \\\"\\\").lower(),\\n        )\\n        for ev in r.get(\\\"evidence\\\", []):\\n            if isinstance(ev, str):\\n                idx = sent_to_idx.get(ev.strip().lower())\\n                if idx is not None:\\n                    all_pred.add((key, idx))\\n\\n    if not all_pred and not all_gold:\\n        return 1.0, 1.0, 1.0\\n\\n    tp = len(all_pred & all_gold)\\n    prec = tp / len(all_pred) if all_pred else 0.0\\n    rec = tp / len(all_gold) if all_gold else 0.0\\n    f1 = 2 * prec * rec / (prec + rec) if (prec + rec) > 0 else 0.0\\n    return prec, rec, f1\\n\\n\\ndef compute_attribution_precision(pred_rels: list[dict]) -> float:\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"Fraction of predicted relations that include non-empty evidence.\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n    if not pred_rels:\\n        return 0.0\\n    with_ev = sum(1 for r in pred_rels if r.get(\\\"evidence\\\") and len(r[\\\"evidence\\\"]) > 0)\\n    return with_ev / len(pred_rels)\\n\\n\\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\\n# Main\\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\\n\\ndef main():\\n    parser = HfArgumentParser((ModelArgs, EvalArgs))\\n    model_args, eval_args = parser.parse_args_into_dataclasses()\\n\\n    # ── Load model ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────\\n    tokenizer = AutoTokenizer.from_pretrained(eval_args.model_path, trust_remote_code=True)\\n    if tokenizer.pad_token is None:\\n        tokenizer.pad_token = tokenizer.eos_token\\n    # Left-padding: causal LMs need prompts right-aligned so generated tokens\\n    # are contiguous at the end of each sequence in the batch.\\n    tokenizer.padding_side = \\\"left\\\"\\n\\n    model_kwargs: dict = {\\\"trust_remote_code\\\": True, \\\"torch_dtype\\\": \\\"auto\\\"}\\n    if model_args.use_flash_attention:\\n        model_kwargs[\\\"attn_implementation\\\"] = \\\"flash_attention_2\\\"\\n\\n    # Handle both full models and PEFT adapters\\n    adapter_config_path = Path(eval_args.model_path) / \\\"adapter_config.json\\\"\\n    if adapter_config_path.exists():\\n        from peft import PeftModel\\n\\n        with open(adapter_config_path) as f:\\n            acfg = json.load(f)\\n        base_name = acfg.get(\\\"base_model_name_or_path\\\", model_args.model_name_or_path)\\n        model = AutoModelForCausalLM.from_pretrained(base_name, **model_kwargs)\\n        model = PeftModel.from_pretrained(model, eval_args.model_path)\\n        model = model.merge_and_unload()\\n    else:\\n        model = AutoModelForCausalLM.from_pretrained(eval_args.model_path, **model_kwargs)\\n\\n    device = \\\"cuda\\\" if torch.cuda.is_available() else \\\"cpu\\\"\\n    model.to(device).eval()\\n\\n    # ── Load data ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────\\n    dataset = load_grpo_dataset(eval_args.eval_file)\\n\\n    # ── Prepare prompts ───────────────────────────────────────────────\\n    all_prompts: list[str] = []\\n    all_gold_rels: list[list[dict]] = []\\n    all_sentences: list[list[str]] = []\\n    for i in range(len(dataset)):\\n        all_gold_rels.append(json.loads(dataset[i][\\\"gold_relations\\\"]))\\n        all_sentences.append(json.loads(dataset[i][\\\"sentences\\\"]))\\n        all_prompts.append(\\n            tokenizer.apply_chat_template(\\n                dataset[i][\\\"prompt\\\"], tokenize=False, add_generation_prompt=True\\n            )\\n        )\\n\\n    # ── Batch generate & evaluate ─────────────────────────────────────\\n    all_re_f1: list[float] = []\\n    all_evi_f1: list[float] = []\\n    all_attr: list[float] = []\\n\", \"stderr\": \"\", \"return_code\": 0}, \"error\": null, \"metadata\": {\"elapsed_seconds\": 0.44}}"}

r.get(\\\"relation\\\", \\\"\\\").lower())\\n        for r in pred_rels\\n    }\\n    gold_set = {\\n        (r[\\\"head\\\"].lower(), r[\\\"tail\\\"].lower(), r[\\\"relation\\\"].lower())\\n        for r in gold_rels\\n    }\\n    if not pred_set and not gold_set:\\n        return 1.0, 1.0, 1.0\\n\\n    tp = len(pred_set & gold_set)\\n    prec = tp / len(pred_set) if pred_set else 0.0\\n    rec = tp / len(gold_set) if gold_set else 0.0\\n    f1 = 2 * prec * rec / (prec + rec) if (prec + rec) > 0 else 0.0\\n    return prec, rec, f1\\n\\n\\ndef compute_evi_f1(\\n    pred_rels: list[dict], gold_rels: list[dict], sentences: list[str]\\n) -> tuple[float, float, float]:\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"Evidence sentence F1 (per-relation, sentence-level).\\n\\n    Matches predicted evidence text back to sentence indices for comparison\\n    with gold evidence_sids.\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n    sent_to_idx: dict[str, int] = {}\\n    for i, s in enumerate(sentences):\\n        sent_to_idx[s.strip().lower()] = i\\n\\n    all_pred: set[tuple] = set()\\n    all_gold: set[tuple] = set()\\n\\n    gold_map: dict[tuple, set[int]] = {}\\n    for r in gold_rels:\\n        key = (r[\\\"head\\\"].lower(), r[\\\"tail\\\"].lower(), r[\\\"relation\\\"].lower())\\n        sids = set(r.get(\\\"evidence_sids\\\", []))\\n        if not sids:\\n            for ev in r.get(\\\"evidence\\\", []):\\n                idx = sent_to_idx.get(ev.strip().lower())\\n                if idx is not None:\\n                    sids.add(idx)\\n        gold_map[key] = sids\\n        for sid in sids:\\n            all_gold.add((key, sid))\\n\\n    for r in pred_rels:\\n        key = (\\n            r.get(\\\"head\\\", \\\"\\\").lower(),\\n            r.get(\\\"tail\\\", \\\"\\\").lower(),\\n            r.get(\\\"relation\\\", \\\"\\\").lower(),\\n        )\\n        for ev in r.get(\\\"evidence\\\", []):\\n            if isinstance(ev, str):\\n                idx = sent_to_idx.get(ev.strip().lower())\\n                if idx is not None:\\n                    all_pred.add((key, idx))\\n\\n    if not all_pred and not all_gold:\\n        return 1.0, 1.0, 1.0\\n\\n    tp = len(all_pred & all_gold)\\n    prec = tp / len(all_pred) if all_pred else 0.0\\n    rec = tp / len(all_gold) if all_gold else 0.0\\n    f1 = 2 * prec * rec / (prec + rec) if (prec + rec) > 0 else 0.0\\n    return prec, rec, f1\\n\\n\\ndef compute_attribution_precision(pred_rels: list[dict]) -> float:\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"Fraction of predicted relations that include non-empty evidence.\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n    if not pred_rels:\\n        return 0.0\\n    with_ev = sum(1 for r in pred_rels if r.get(\\\"evidence\\\") and len(r[\\\"evidence\\\"]) > 0)\\n    return with_ev / len(pred_rels)\\n\\n\\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\\n# Main\\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\\n\\ndef main():\\n    parser = HfArgumentParser((ModelArgs, EvalArgs))\\n    model_args, eval_args = parser.parse_args_into_dataclasses()\\n\\n    # ── Load model ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────\\n    tokenizer = AutoTokenizer.from_pretrained(eval_args.model_path, trust_remote_code=True)\\n    if tokenizer.pad_token is None:\\n        tokenizer.pad_token = tokenizer.eos_token\\n    # Left-padding: causal LMs need prompts right-aligned so generated tokens\\n    # are contiguous at the end of each sequence in the batch.\\n    tokenizer.padding_side = \\\"left\\\"\\n\\n    model_kwargs: dict = {\\\"trust_remote_code\\\": True, \\\"torch_dtype\\\": \\\"auto\\\"}\\n    if model_args.use_flash_attention:\\n        model_kwargs[\\\"attn_implementation\\\"] = \\\"flash_attention_2\\\"\\n\\n    # Handle both full models and PEFT adapters\\n    adapter_config_path = Path(eval_args.model_path) / \\\"adapter_config.json\\\"\\n    if adapter_config_path.exists():\\n        from peft import PeftModel\\n\\n        with open(adapter_config_path) as f:\\n            acfg = json.load(f)\\n        base_name = acfg.get(\\\"base_model_name_or_path\\\", model_args.model_name_or_path)\\n        model = AutoModelForCausalLM.from_pretrained(base_name, **model_kwargs)\\n        model = PeftModel.from_pretrained(model, eval_args.model_path)\\n        model = model.merge_and_unload()\\n    else:\\n        model = AutoModelForCausalLM.from_pretrained(eval_args.model_path, **model_kwargs)\\n\\n    device = \\\"cuda\\\" if torch.cuda.is_available() else \\\"cpu\\\"\\n    model.to(device).eval()\\n\\n    # ── Load data ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────\\n    dataset = load_grpo_dataset(eval_args.eval_file)\\n\\n    # ── Prepare prompts ───────────────────────────────────────────────\\n    all_prompts: list[str] = []\\n    all_gold_rels: list[list[dict]] = []\\n    all_sentences: list[list[str]] = []\\n    for i in range(len(dataset)):\\n        all_gold_rels.append(json.loads(dataset[i][\\\"gold_relations\\\"]))\\n        all_sentences.append(json.loads(dataset[i][\\\"sentences\\\"]))\\n        all_prompts.append(\\n            tokenizer.apply_chat_template(\\n                dataset[i][\\\"prompt\\\"], tokenize=False, add_generation_prompt=True\\n            )\\n        )\\n\\n    # ── Batch generate & evaluate ```text
Excellent! Now I have the full picture:

**EvalArgs.temperature default = 0.0** → greedy decoding

In evaluate.py line 186: `do_sample = eval_args.temperature > 0` → with default temperature=0.0, `do_sample = False` → **greedy decoding**.

The gen_kwargs will be: `{"max_new_tokens": 2048, "do_sample": False}`.

The warning about "temperature, top_p, top_k" being ignored makes sense now — these are set in the model's `generation_config.json` (temperature=0.6, top_k=20, top_p=0.95) but since `do_sample=False`, sampling parameters are irrelevant and thus "not valid". This is benign — greedy decoding is appropriate for evaluation.
 
Now I'm looking at how the chat template handles thinking mode. When building prompts at line 162-164, the code applies the chat template with `add_generation_prompt=True` but doesn't pass `enable_thinking`. Looking at the OLD template from sft_eval_v4, it checks whether `enable_thinking` is defined and false to determine whether to include the thinking tags.

Since `enable_thinking` isn't passed, it's undefined, so the condition fails and no empty thinking block gets injected—the model will naturally generate thinking tags. But the NEW template always injects an empty `&lt;think>&lt;/think>` block whenever `add_generation_prompt=True`, which effectively disables thinking mode entirely.

So sft_eval_v4 is running with thinking enabled, meaning the model wastes tokens on internal reasoning before outputting JSON. With a 2048 token limit, this could consume a significant portion of the budget and truncate the actual output, explaining the low RE-F1 of 0.28. The fact that it's still producing parseable JSON is surprising given the token constraints. This suggests the template fix happened after these results were collected, making sft_eval_v4's metrics potentially unreliable as a baseline.

Now I'm checking whether the tokenizer's `skip_special_tokens=True` parameter would strip the `&lt;think>` tags. In Qwen3, these are likely regular tokens rather than special tokens, so they'd remain in the decoded output. That means `parse_json_output` would receive the thinking block alongside the JSON, which could cause parsing failures if the function isn't designed to handle that format.

The RE-F1 score of 0.28 suggests some predictions are parsing successfully, but thinking mode might be causing failures in others. The core issue is that the template fix happened after the eval started, so sft_eval_v4 is still running with the old template where thinking mode wasn't disabled. This could artificially depress the results, and re-running with the corrected template would likely show improvement.

For the worker's changes, they identified the thinking mode problem and fixed the template by removing the conditional logic to always inject an empty think block. They also modified the config, rewards, and training pipeline files to prepare for GRPO. The generation config test ran on GPU 1, but the critical gap is that sft_eval_v4 wasn't restarted with the fixed template, and GPU 1 is now sitting idle. I should check what the rewards and training modifications actually do to understand the full scope of their work.

<details><summary>Logged reasoning block</summary>

````text
Excellent! Line 61 in rewards.py:
```python
text = re.sub(r'&lt;think>.*?&lt;/think>', '', text, flags=re.DOTALL).strip()
```

The `parse_json_output` function already strips `&lt;think>...&lt;/think>` blocks from the output! This means even though sft_eval_v4 is using the old template (thinking mode enabled), the parsing correctly removes the thinking content before extracting JSON.

So the eval results should be valid UNLESS:
1. The thinking consumes so many tokens that the actual JSON answer gets truncated at `max_new_tokens=2048`


2. The thinking interferes with generation quality somehow

With a 2048 token budget and thinking potentially eating 200-1000+ tokens, there's real competition for the remaining space needed for JSON output. If a sample requires extensive JSON with many relations and evidence, truncation becomes a genuine risk that could depress the RE-F1 score.

The thinking mode issue is mitigated by the stripping logic, but token budget constraints remain a legitimate concern for longer outputs. Now let me check what else is in rewards.py—

specifically whether the thinking stripping was added recently by the fix-thinking-retest worker. The file was modified after sft_eval_v2.log was created, which suggests the worker added this handling for thinking mode output.

Actually, there's a timing issue here. sft_eval_v4 started at 05:38 and imported parse_json_output at that moment, but rewards.py was modified after the startup. Python imports are loaded at import time, so if the file changed after sft_eval_v4 loaded it, the running process would still be using the old version without the stripping logic.

The comment about stripping Qwen3 thinking tags as a "fallback safety" measure suggests this was added by the worker, which means sft_eval_v4 wouldn't actually have access to that code. But I can't definitively tell from the file timestamps whether the stripping was in the original version or added later.

What's interesting is that the RE-F1 score of 0.28 isn't catastrophically low — if thinking mode was completely breaking the output parsing, I'd expect something much closer to zero. This suggests either the old rewards.py already had the stripping logic, the model doesn't always trigger thinking mode, or some outputs are parsing correctly despite containing think blocks. I'll note this as a potential issue but acknowledge the score indicates partial success.

Now looking at what the worker actually did: they disabled thinking mode by injecting an empty think block into the chat template, backed up the original files, updated the config with Qwen3-specific parameters like top_k and top_p, added think stripping to the rewards parsing function, and set up the GRPO pipeline before running a generation config test on GPU 1.

The key issue is timing — the template fix went live after sft_eval_v4 had already started, so that evaluation is still running with the old template and potentially without think stripping in rewards.py, which could skew the results. GPU 1 is now idle after the generation test finished, and posthoc_nli_v1 hasn't restarted yet. The template approach itself is correct for disabling Qwen3's reasoning mode, and the GRPO setup is moving forward with the generation parameters configured.

With the NEW template, this kwarg is ignored (since thinking is always disabled). Either way, GRPO training will have thinking disabled — the worker covered both approaches for robustness.

But the key issue remains: **evaluate.py does NOT pass `enable_thinking=False`** in its `apply_chat_template` call (line 162-164), and sft_eval_v4 loaded the OLD template where this matters.


The NEW template would actually solve this for future eval runs automatically, even without changing evaluate.py. I'm checking if the generation config test produced any output — PID 58729 ran as a one-liner, so its results went to the SSH session rather than a log file and I can't retrieve them. Now I'm ready to compile my comprehensive review of the worker's fix.

The worker correctly identified and fixed the thinking mode issue by modifying the chat template to unconditionally disable thinking, also added `&lt;think>` stripping to rewards.py as a safety measure, and prepared the GRPO pipeline with updated configs. However, sft_eval_v4 loaded the old template so those results may still be affected by thinking mode, and GPU 1 is now idle with no productive work queued up.

{
  "text": "## 审查报告 — worker_ssh_fix-thinking-retest\n\n**结论**: WARNING\n\n### Worker 做了什么\n1. **修复 Qwen3 thinking mode**：修改 `outputs/sft/merged/chat_template.jinja`，删除 `enable_thinking` 条件判断，改为无条件注入空 `&lt;think>\\n\\n&lt;/think>` 块禁用 thinking（旧模板只在 `enable_thinking=False` 时禁用，evaluate.py 未传此参数）\n2. **rewards.py 增加 `&lt;think>` 剥离**：`parse_json_output` 加了 `re.sub(r'&lt;think>.*?&lt;/think>', '', text, flags=re.DOTALL)` 作为安全网\n3. **GRPO 管线准备**：config.py 更新 GRPOArgs（top_k=20, top_p=0.95 匹配 Qwen3 默认）；train_grpo.py line 77 设 `chat_template_kwargs={\"enable_thinking\": False}`\n4. **GPU 1 上跑了生成配置对比测试**（TRL defaults vs model defaults），已完成\n\n### 发现\n\n- [severity: warning] **sft_eval_v4 使用旧模板运行**：eval 进程 05:38 启动，模板 06:57 才修改。旧模板下 `apply_chat_template(add_generation_prompt=True)` 不传 `enable_thinking=False` → thinking mode 未被禁用 → 模型生成时先输出 `&lt;think>...&lt;/think>` 再输出 JSON。两个后果：(a) thinking 消耗 `max_new_tokens=2048` 额度，长输出可能被截断；(b) 旧版 rewards.py 是否有 `&lt;think>` 剥离不确定（文件在 eval 启动后才改），如果没有，`parse_json_output` 会解析失败。当前 RE-F1=0.28 低于预期可能与此有关。**建议 eval 完成后用修复后的模板重跑一次 eval 以获得可靠锚点。**\n\n- [severity: warning] **GPU 1 持续空闲**：调试脚本已完成，GPU 1 利用率 0%/0MB。posthoc_nli_v1 失败后未重启（MVP Step 3）。已连续两次审查观察到 GPU 1 空转。\n\n- [severity: info] **thinking mode 修复方案正确**：无条件注入空 think 块是禁用 Qwen3 thinking 的标准做法；train_grpo.py 双保险（模板 + kwargs）；rewards.py 增加 fallback 剥离。GRPO 管线三个文件的修改为 Step 4 做好了准备。\n\n- [severity: info] **generation_config.json 确认**：模型默认 `do_sample=true, temperature=0.6, top_k=20, top_p=0.95`。evaluate.py 用 `temperature=0.0`（greedy），生成配置 warning 是正常的（采样参数在 greedy 模式下被忽略），不影响结果。\n\n### 建议\n1. **sft_eval_v4 完成后立即用修复后的模板重跑 eval**（sft_eval_v5），作为可靠的 SFT baseline 锚点\n2. **GPU 1 应立即投入工作**——重启 posthoc_nli 或并行跑其他 MVP 步骤",
